# AI in biology: institutions, models and resources, people, and claims about AI changing biology

Generated from `pack.json` by `node research/ai-in-biology/tools.mjs render`; do not edit by hand. Candidate research pack outside application data. Machine-prepared and audited by the same model family; not editorially accepted; no human review; cross-family check not yet run. Check date 2026-10-30. Method and limits: [README](README.md) and [scope](scope.md).

Each record states what a document says, dated by the kind of date the source gives. A paper’s statement of its contribution is the authors’; a company’s statement of results, funding or a trial is the company’s; an announced partnership stays announced; a role is dated by the document that states it. A proposition record says that a text states it, never that it is true.

Dated events across strands, from the records below and from the dates of the pack’s document versions. | 1987-03-26 | published           | Claims about AI and biology | The Nature review "Knowledge-based prediction of protein structures and the design of novel molecules" (Blundell, Sibanda, Sternberg and Thornton, 26 March 1987) states that knowledge-based prediction techniques are of value in predicting receptor structures to aid the design of drugs.                                                                                     | `aib-arg-p1-blundell-1987`                                   |

### Institute for Protein Design, University of Washington

#### Founding, roles and awards

- **The Institute for Protein Design's about page states that it was "Founded in 2012"; UW Medicine's 2019 fact sheet states that it "was established at the University of Washington School of Medicine in 2012".** `aib-labs-ipd-founding-stated`
  2012 · event, no basis stated
  _Date:_ Year as stated; the IPD post announcing the establishment is dated April 13, 2012 (aib-labs-ipd-mission-2012).
  _Limits:_ A stated founding year; no formal establishment instrument was retrieved.

  > “Founded in 2012, the Institute for Protein Design”  
  > [About Us](https://www.ipd.uw.edu/about/), opening section
  > “The Institute for Protein Design was established at the University of Washington School of Medicine in 2012 and is housed within the school's Department of Biochemistry.”  
  > [Institute for Protein Design Audacious project facts](https://newsroom.uw.edu/news-releases/institute-protein-design-audacious-project-facts/), section "What is the Institute for Protein Design?"

- **UW Medicine's April 2019 fact sheet describes the Institute for Protein Design as established at the University of Washington School of Medicine and housed within its Department of Biochemistry.** `aib-labs-ipd-unit-of-uw`
  2019-04-16 · snapshot
  _Date:_ Source-dated snapshot.
  _Limits:_ An institutional placement as stated; not a separate legal entity or an ownership relation.
  > “The Institute for Protein Design was established at the University of Washington School of Medicine in 2012 and is housed within the school's Department of Biochemistry.”  
  > [Institute for Protein Design Audacious project facts](https://newsroom.uw.edu/news-releases/institute-protein-design-audacious-project-facts/), section "What is the Institute for Protein Design?"

#### What the text states

- **The IPD's post of 13 April 2012 announcing its establishment states: "Our mission is to design synthetic proteins that address challenges in medicine, energy, and technology."** `aib-labs-ipd-mission-2012`
  2012-04-13 · published
  _Date:_ Dated by the post byline.
  _Limits:_ A stated mission at establishment. The current about page states a differently worded mission (aib-labs-ipd-mission-2026); the change is recorded as two statements, not as a dated revision.

  > “Our mission is to design synthetic proteins that address challenges in medicine, energy, and technology.”  
  > [UW establishes the Institute for Protein Design](https://www.ipd.uw.edu/2012/04/uw-to-establish-institute-for-protein-design/), paragraph 1

- **The IPD's about page (as served on 2026-10-30) states: "Our mission is to create new proteins that address current challenges across medicine, technology, and sustainability."** `aib-labs-ipd-mission-2026`
  2026-10-30 · snapshot
  _Date:_ Undated page; snapshot at retrieval.
  _Limits:_ A stated mission; when the wording changed from the 2012 statement is not established.
  > “Our mission is to create new proteins that address current challenges across medicine, technology, and sustainability.”  
  > [About Us](https://www.ipd.uw.edu/about/), opening section

### Chris Gibson

#### Founding, roles and awards

- **Recursion's IPO prospectus (15 April 2021) names Christopher Gibson as the company's co-founder.** `aib-co-gibson-recursion-cofounded`
  2013-11 · event, no basis stated
  _Date:_ Founding month as stated in Recursion's IPO prospectus of 15 April 2021 ("since the company's founding in November 2013"; its Corporate Information dates the company's formation to November 2013); no day is given and no separate date of Gibson's participation is stated.
  _Limits:_ Co-founding is as the prospectus states it; the same text names Blake Borgeson as a co-founder too, and no complete founder roster is implied.
  > “Christopher Gibson, Ph.D., is our co-founder and Chief Executive Officer”  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Management, "Executive Officers"
  > the same sentence continues that he has held the role since the company's founding in November 2013. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Management, "Executive Officers", same sentence

### Recursion Pharmaceuticals

#### Founding, roles and awards

- **Recursion's IPO prospectus (15 April 2021) states that the company was formed in Delaware as a limited liability company in November 2013 under the name Recursion Pharmaceuticals, LLC.** `aib-co-recursion-founding-stated`
  2013-11 · event, no basis stated
  _Date:_ Month as stated by the prospectus; no day is given. The prospectus also dates a conversion to a Delaware corporation to September 2016.
  _Limits:_ The prospectus states a formation month as a limited liability company; it does not establish a founder roster, which other records cover.

  > “We were formed in Delaware as a limited liability company in November 2013 under the name Recursion Pharmaceuticals, LLC. In September 2016, we converted to a Delaware corporation and subsequently changed our name to Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc.”  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Prospectus summary, "Corporate Information"

- **Recursion's IPO prospectus (15 April 2021) names Christopher Gibson as its Chief Executive Officer and states that he had held the role since the company's founding in November 2013.** `aib-co-gibson-recursion-ceo-2021`
  2021-04-15 · snapshot
  _Date:_ A role stated in the prospectus of this date; the start as stated (November 2013) is the company's statement. No continuing appointment is implied.
  _Limits:_ A historical role snapshot. The record does not establish the end of the role or any later title.
  > “Christopher Gibson, Ph.D., is our co-founder and Chief Executive Officer”  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Management, "Executive Officers"
  > the same sentence continues that he has held the role since the company's founding in November 2013. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Management, "Executive Officers", same sentence

#### Funding, partnerships and acquisitions

- **Recursion announced on 7 December 2021 a collaboration with Roche and Genentech in neuroscience and an oncology indication, under which it would receive an upfront payment of $150 million, and stated that Roche and Genentech may initiate up to 40 programs, each of which could yield more than $300 million in milestones if successfully developed and commercialized.** `aib-co-recursion-roche-genentech-2021`
  2021-12-07 · announced · proposed predicate `partnership_announced`
  _Date:_ Date of Recursion's press release.
  _Limits:_ Announced terms as Recursion states them. The milestone figures are conditional and per programme; they are not totalled, and the record does not establish payments received. Genentech is a party with Roche (the release names Genentech as a member of the Roche Group).

  > “Under the terms of the agreement, Recursion will receive an upfront payment of $150 million and is eligible for additional performance-based research milestones. Under the collaboration, Roche and Genentech (combined) may initiate up to 40 programs”  
  > [Recursion Announces Transformational Collaboration with Roche and Genentech in Neuroscience and Oncology](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521349657/d242203dex991.htm), first paragraph
  > “each of which, if successfully developed and commercialized, could yield more than $300M in development, commercialization and net sales milestones for Recursion”  
  > [Recursion Announces Transformational Collaboration with Roche and Genentech in Neuroscience and Oncology](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521349657/d242203dex991.htm), first paragraph

- **Recursion announced on 12 July 2023 plans to develop AI foundation models for biology and chemistry which, in collaboration with NVIDIA, it intends to optimize and distribute to biotechnology companies using NVIDIA cloud services, with possible release on NVIDIA's BioNeMo.** `aib-co-recursion-nvidia-collaboration-2023`
  2023-07-12 · announced · proposed predicate `partnership_announced`
  _Date:_ Date of Recursion's press release.
  _Limits:_ An announced collaboration and stated intentions ("plans", "intends", "possible"); the record does not establish that any model was trained, licensed or released.

  > “Recursion also announced plans to accelerate development of its AI foundation models for biology and chemistry, which, in collaboration with NVIDIA, it intends to optimize and distribute to biotechnology companies using NVIDIA cloud services.”  
  > [Recursion Announces Collaboration and $50 Million Investment from NVIDIA to Accelerate Groundbreaking Foundation Models in AI-Enabled Drug Discovery](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000160183023000050/ex991-rxrxxnvidiapr.htm), first paragraph
  > “for possible commercial license/release on BioNeMo, NVIDIA’s cloud service for generative AI in drug discovery.”  
  > [Recursion Announces Collaboration and $50 Million Investment from NVIDIA to Accelerate Groundbreaking Foundation Models in AI-Enabled Drug Discovery](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000160183023000050/ex991-rxrxxnvidiapr.htm), third paragraph

- **Recursion stated on 20 November 2024 that its business combination with Exscientia had been completed, with Exscientia becoming a wholly owned subsidiary of Recursion.** `aib-co-recursion-acquired-exscientia-2024`
  2024-11-20 · announced
  _Date:_ Date of Recursion's press release reporting completion. The Form 8-K to which the release is Exhibit 99.3 (accession 0001601830-24-000217, filed 2024-11-20) gives 2024-11-19 as its period of report (date of earliest event reported), under Item 2.01 Completion of Acquisition or Disposition of Assets, as its EDGAR filing index page shows (retrieved 2026-10-30); completion may therefore have taken effect on 19 November 2024. The 8-K body was not retrieved (HTTP 403).
  _Limits:_ Recursion's statement of completion, filed as an exhibit to its Form 8-K; the record does not state the consideration paid at closing or the legal steps (the 2024-08-08 announcement describes a court-sanctioned scheme).
  > “The business combination of two AI-powered drug discovery and development companies, Recursion (Nasdaq: RXRX) and Exscientia has been completed, with Exscientia becoming a wholly owned subsidiary of Recursion”  
  > [Recursion and Exscientia, two leaders in the AI drug discovery space, have officially combined to advance the industrialization of drug discovery](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000160183024000217/exhibit993-closingprexai_r.htm), first paragraph

#### What the text states

- **Recursion's IPO prospectus (15 April 2021) states that the company had four clinical stage programs and 33 additional programs in preclinical development.** `aib-co-recursion-clinical-programs-2021`
  2021-04-15 · published
  _Date:_ Prospectus date; the statement is a snapshot of the company's pipeline as it describes it.
  _Limits:_ The company's own count of its programs at the prospectus date; it does not establish trial phases, trial registrations or outcomes, and it does not state that each candidate was discovered by machine learning.

  > “We have four clinical stage programs and 33 additional programs in various stages of preclinical development.”  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Risk factors

- **Recursion's prospectus dated 15 April 2021 states an initial public offering of 24,242,424 shares of Class A common stock at $18.00 per share, approved for listing on the Nasdaq Global Select Market under the symbol RXRX.** `aib-co-recursion-ipo-2021`
  2021-04-15 · published
  _Date:_ Prospectus date; the prospectus states delivery of shares on or about April 20, 2021. Trading and closing dates are not recorded here.
  _Limits:_ The prospectus states the offering terms; it is not evidence of the shares sold after any over-allotment option, the proceeds realised or the closing.

  > “This is the initial public offering of shares of Class A common stock of Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. We are offering 24,242,424 shares of our Class A common stock. The initial public offering price is $18.00 per share.”  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Prospectus cover
  > the cover states that the Class A common stock has been approved for listing on the Nasdaq Global Select Market under the symbol RXRX. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Prospectus cover

- **The letter from Recursion's co-founder and CEO in its IPO prospectus (15 April 2021) states the company's mission as "to Decode Biology to Radically Improve Lives".** `aib-co-recursion-mission-2021`
  2021-04-15 · published
  _Date:_ Prospectus date.
  _Limits:_ A company's statement of its own mission; it says nothing about results.

  > “Our mission is to Decode Biology to Radically Improve Lives.”  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), A Letter from our Co-Founder and CEO

- **Recursion's IPO prospectus (15 April 2021) describes the company as a clinical-stage biotechnology company decoding biology by integrating biology, chemistry, automation, data science and engineering, with the goal of "industrializing drug discovery", and names the Recursion OS, including the Recursion Map, which it describes as "a suite of custom software, algorithms, and machine learning tools", as central to that mission.** `aib-co-recursion-purpose-2021`
  2021-04-15 · published
  _Date:_ Prospectus date.
  _Limits:_ The company's description of its approach; the dataset size and "one of the world's largest" characterisations in the same passage are the company's and are not recorded as facts.

  > “We are a clinical-stage biotechnology company decoding biology by integrating technological innovations across biology, chemistry, automation, data science, and engineering, with the goal of radically improving the lives of patients and industrializing drug discovery.”  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Prospectus summary, "Overview"
  > “Central to our mission is the Recursion Operating System, or Recursion OS”  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Prospectus summary, "Overview", next sentence
  > “a suite of custom software, algorithms, and machine learning tools”  
  > [Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. prospectus (Rule 424(b)(4)), initial public offering of Class A common stock](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312521119404/d89478d424b4.htm), Prospectus summary, "Overview", same sentence

- **Recursion and Exscientia announced on 8 August 2024 a definitive agreement to combine, under which Exscientia shareholders would receive 0.7729 shares of Recursion Class A common stock per Exscientia share, Recursion and Exscientia shareholders would own about 74% and 26% of the combined company, and closing was expected by early 2025.** `aib-co-recursion-exscientia-agreement-2024`
  2024-08-08 · announced
  _Date:_ Date of the joint press release.
  _Limits:_ Announced terms and an expected closing, subject to conditions stated in the release; completion is a separate record. The 74%/26% split is stated as based on the fixed exchange ratio and assuming no additional issuance by either company before closing; the closing expectation is stated as subject to the satisfaction or waiver of the closing conditions.
  > “Exscientia shareholders will receive 0.7729 shares of Recursion Class A common stock for each Exscientia ordinary share they own, with fractional shares paid in cash.”  
  > [Recursion and Exscientia Enter Definitive Agreement to Create A Global Technology-Enabled Drug Discovery Leader with End-to-End Capabilities](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312524196505/d856792dex991.htm), Transaction Details
  > “Recursion shareholders will own approximately 74% and Exscientia shareholders will own approximately 26% of the combined company”  
  > [Recursion and Exscientia Enter Definitive Agreement to Create A Global Technology-Enabled Drug Discovery Leader with End-to-End Capabilities](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312524196505/d856792dex991.htm), Transaction Details
  > “the transaction is expected to close by early 2025.”  
  > [Recursion and Exscientia Enter Definitive Agreement to Create A Global Technology-Enabled Drug Discovery Leader with End-to-End Capabilities](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1601830/000119312524196505/d856792dex991.htm), Transaction Details

#### What the text states

  2022-07-07 · published

  > [AI4Science to empower the fifth paradigm of scientific discovery](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/blog/ai4science-to-empower-the-fifth-paradigm-of-scientific-discovery/), section on the fifth paradigm

### Assembly of protein tertiary structures from fragments with similar local sequences using simulated annealing and Bayesian scoring functions

#### What the text states

- **Simons et al. (Journal of Molecular Biology, 25 April 1997) state that they explore the ability of a simple simulated annealing procedure to assemble native-like structures from fragments of unrelated protein structures with similar local sequences, using Bayesian scoring functions.** `aib-mod-rosetta-1997-method`
  1997-04-25 · published
  _Date:_ Publication date as given in the PubMed record of the paper.
  _Limits:_ The authors' statement of their own study, read in the abstract as reproduced by PubMed; the journal expression was not retrieved. The abstract does not use the name Rosetta; the full paper was not read.
  > “We explore the ability of a simple simulated annealing procedure to assemble native-like structures from fragments of unrelated protein structures with similar local sequences using Bayesian scoring functions.”  
  > [Assembly of protein tertiary structures from fragments with similar local sequences using simulated annealing and Bayesian scoring functions (PubMed record)](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/9149153/), abstract as reproduced in the PubMed record

#### What the text states

- **The author list of Cooper et al. (Nature, 2010) credits "Foldit players" as a collective author alongside the named researchers.** `aib-mod-foldit-collective-author`

  _Limits:_ A credit as printed; it names no individuals and establishes no individual authorship.

  > [Predicting protein structures with a multiplayer online game](https://www.nature.com/articles/nature09304?error=cookies_not_supported&code=59d14afc-bb1e-43cc-a8b5-a8325a90d108), article page, author list

- **Cooper et al. (Nature 466, issue of 5 August 2010) state that they describe Foldit, "a multiplayer online game that engages non-scientists in solving hard prediction problems", in which players interact with protein structures using direct manipulation tools and versions of algorithms from the Rosetta structure prediction methodology.** `aib-mod-foldit-contribution`
  2010-08-05 · published
  _Date:_ Issue date on the article page; the page states no separate online publication date.
  _Limits:_ The authors' statement of what their paper describes; read in the abstract, the main text being behind a paywall.

  > “Here we describe Foldit, a multiplayer online game that engages non-scientists in solving hard prediction problems.”

  > “Foldit players interact with protein structures using direct manipulation tools and user-friendly versions of algorithms from the Rosetta structure prediction methodology4”  
  > [Predicting protein structures with a multiplayer online game](https://www.nature.com/articles/nature09304?error=cookies_not_supported&code=59d14afc-bb1e-43cc-a8b5-a8325a90d108), abstract

- **Cooper et al. (Nature, issue of 5 August 2010) state that top-ranked Foldit players "excel at solving challenging structure refinement problems" in which substantial backbone rearrangements are necessary, and that players working collaboratively develop new strategies and algorithms.** `aib-mod-foldit-result`
  2010-08-05 · published

  _Limits:_ The authors' report of their own study, read in the abstract only; the comparison set and measures are in the main text, which was not retrieved.
  > “We show that top-ranked Foldit players excel at solving challenging structure refinement problems in which substantial backbone rearrangements are necessary to achieve the burial of hydrophobic residues.”  
  > [Predicting protein structures with a multiplayer online game](https://www.nature.com/articles/nature09304?error=cookies_not_supported&code=59d14afc-bb1e-43cc-a8b5-a8325a90d108), abstract

### Critical Assessment of Methods of Protein Structure Prediction (CASP) – Round XIII

#### What the text states

- **Kryshtafovych et al. (Proteins, 23 October 2019) state that one group, A7D from DeepMind, appear to have very successfully deployed the technique of deriving residue-pair potentials and had the most accurate results overall in CASP13.** `aib-mod-casp13-a7d-result`
  2019-10-23 · published
  _Date:_ Date given in PMC's "Published in final edited form" line for the organisers' overview paper (kind not stated).
  _Limits:_ The CASP organisers' statement in their overview of the round (the paper distinguishes the independent assessors, whose analyses are separate papers) about one participating group under its CASP group name, not DeepMind's own account and not a record of any particular score. The hedge "appear to have" is the authors' own. Read in the PMC author manuscript; 23 October 2019 is the date in PMC's "Published in final edited form" line, its kind not stated; issue December 2019.

  > “One group, A7D from DeepMind … appear to have very successfully deployed this technique, and had the most accurate results overall.”  
  > [Critical Assessment of Methods of Protein Structure Prediction (CASP) – Round XIII](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6927249/), section "TEMPLATE FREE MODELING" (PMC author manuscript)

- **Kryshtafovych et al. (Proteins, 23 October 2019) state that the further jump in CASP13 contact-prediction performance is the result of the effective deployment of deep learning methods, where the gains in CASP11 and CASP12 came from more sophisticated statistical models and largely conventional machine learning.** `aib-mod-casp13-contact-precision`
  2019-10-23 · published
  _Date:_ Date given in PMC's "Published in final edited form" line for the organisers' overview paper (kind not stated).
  _Limits:_ The CASP organisers' attribution across rounds, in their overview of CASP13, as stated in a figure discussion; the underlying measurements are not reproduced here.

  > “In CASPs 11 and 12 progress is the result of more sophisticated statistical models, together with largely conventional machine learning. The further jump in CASP13 is the result of the effective deployment of deep learning methods.”  
  > [Critical Assessment of Methods of Protein Structure Prediction (CASP) – Round XIII](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6927249/), discussion of contact-prediction progress across CASP rounds

- **Kryshtafovych et al. (Proteins, 23 October 2019), reporting on CASP13 as its organisers, state that the experiment held in 2018 saw dramatic progress in structure modeling without use of structural templates, and that progress was driven by the successful application of deep learning techniques to predict inter-residue distances.** `aib-mod-casp13-deep-learning`
  2019-10-23 · published
  _Date:_ Date given in PMC's "Published in final edited form" line for the organisers' overview paper (kind not stated). CASP13 itself was held in 2018 as the paper states.
  _Limits:_ The CASP organisers' summary in their overview of the round (the independent assessors' analyses are separate papers), in the author-manuscript expression. It attributes progress to a class of methods, not to any one group, and it is not a measurement reproduced here.
  > “The most recent experiment (CASP13 held in 2018) saw dramatic progress in structure modeling without use of structural templates”  
  > [Critical Assessment of Methods of Protein Structure Prediction (CASP) – Round XIII](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6927249/), abstract
  > “Progress was driven by the successful application of deep learning techniques to predict inter-residue distances.”  
  > [Critical Assessment of Methods of Protein Structure Prediction (CASP) – Round XIII](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6927249/), abstract

### Improved protein structure prediction using potentials from deep learning

#### What the text states

- **Senior et al. (Nature, 15 January 2020) state in their code availability section that source code for the distogram, reference distogram and torsion prediction neural networks, together with the neural network weights and input data for the CASP13 targets, are available for research and non-commercial use.** `aib-mod-af-casp13-code`
  2020-01-15 · published
  _Date:_ Date of the paper making the statement.
  _Limits:_ What the paper's code availability section states, read on the article page as served on 2026-10-30 (published and version of record 15 January 2020; the page as originally served was not read); not a check that the repository is still reachable or that the terms are unchanged.

  > “Source code for the distogram, reference distogram and torsion prediction neural networks, together with the neural network weights and input data for the CASP13 targets are available for research and non-commercial use”  
  > [Improved protein structure prediction using potentials from deep learning](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-019-1923-7?error=cookies_not_supported&code=7e8bb4c1-5017-40d9-a9ca-1aa62023d504), code availability

- **Senior et al. (Nature, published online 15 January 2020) state that they train a neural network to make accurate predictions of the distances between pairs of residues, which convey more information about the structure than contact predictions, and that the resulting system is named AlphaFold.** `aib-mod-af-casp13-contribution`
  2020-01-15 · published
  _Date:_ Online publication date on the article page.
  _Limits:_ The authors' own statement of what their paper reports; not an independent assessment. The system named here is the one entered in CASP13, distinct from the 2021 AlphaFold paper's redesigned system.

  > “Here we show that we can train a neural network to make accurate predictions of the distances between pairs of residues, which convey more information about the structure than contact predictions.”  
  > [Improved protein structure prediction using potentials from deep learning](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-019-1923-7?error=cookies_not_supported&code=7e8bb4c1-5017-40d9-a9ca-1aa62023d504), abstract
  > “The resulting system, named AlphaFold, achieves high accuracy, even for sequences with fewer homologous sequences.”  
  > [Improved protein structure prediction using potentials from deep learning](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-019-1923-7?error=cookies_not_supported&code=7e8bb4c1-5017-40d9-a9ca-1aa62023d504), abstract

- **Senior et al. (Nature, 15 January 2020) report that in CASP13 AlphaFold created high-accuracy structures, with template modelling scores of 0.7 or higher, for 24 out of 43 free modelling domains, whereas the next best method achieved such accuracy for only 14 out of 43 domains.** `aib-mod-af-casp13-domains`
  2020-01-15 · published
  _Date:_ Date of the paper reporting the result.
  _Limits:_ The authors' report of their own CASP13 entry, published after the assessment; the CASP organisers' statements about CASP13, in their overview of the round, are recorded separately. Comparison is to the next best method in that round on that domain set only.
  > “AlphaFold created high-accuracy structures … for 24 out of 43 free modelling domains, whereas the next best method, which used sampling and contact information, achieved such accuracy for only 14 out of 43 domains.”  
  > [Improved protein structure prediction using potentials from deep learning](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-019-1923-7?error=cookies_not_supported&code=7e8bb4c1-5017-40d9-a9ca-1aa62023d504), abstract

  > “whereas the next best performing method had a median backbone accuracy of 2.8 Å r.m.s.d.95”

- **Jumper et al. (Nature, 15 July 2021) state in their code availability section that source code for the AlphaFold model, trained weights and an inference script are available under an open-source license.** `aib-mod-af2-code-open`
  2021-07-15 · published
  _Date:_ Date of the paper making the statement.

  > “Source code for the AlphaFold model, trained weights and inference script are available under an open-source license”

- **Jumper et al. (Nature, 15 July 2021) state as a further limitation that AlphaFold is much weaker for proteins that have few intra-chain or homotypic contacts compared to the number of heterotypic contacts, which they say typically occurs for bridging domains within larger complexes.** `aib-mod-af2-heterotypic-limit`
  2021-07-15 · published
  _Date:_ Date of the paper stating the limitation.
  _Limits:_ The authors' own observation about their system, referred by them to a companion paper that was not retrieved.

  > “The other substantial limitation that we have observed is that AlphaFold is much weaker for proteins that have few intra-chain or homotypic contacts compared to the number of heterotypic contacts”  
  > [Highly accurate protein structure prediction with AlphaFold](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-021-03819-2?error=cookies_not_supported&code=0e83ce10-f6de-49eb-ba4f-f89234f590f4), main text, discussion of limitations

- **Jumper et al. (Nature, 15 July 2021) state as a limitation that the model uses multiple sequence alignments and that accuracy decreases substantially when the median alignment depth is less than around 30 sequences.** `aib-mod-af2-msa-depth-limit`
  2021-07-15 · published
  _Date:_ Date of the paper stating the limitation.
  _Limits:_ A limitation the authors state for their own system and evaluation sets; the threshold is theirs and is not an independent measurement.
  > “The model uses MSAs and the accuracy decreases substantially when the median alignment depth is less than around 30 sequences”  
  > [Highly accurate protein structure prediction with AlphaFold](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-021-03819-2?error=cookies_not_supported&code=0e83ce10-f6de-49eb-ba4f-f89234f590f4), main text, discussion of factors that limit applicability

### Accurate prediction of protein structures and interactions using a three-track neural network

#### What the text states

- **Baek et al. (Science, 15 July 2021) state in their abstract that they make the method available to the scientific community to speed biological research.** `aib-mod-rosettafold-availability`
  2021-07-15 · published
  _Date:_ Date of the paper making the statement.
  _Limits:_ A statement of intent about availability, read in the abstract of the author manuscript (PMC7612213) as served on 2026-10-30; the Science expression was not read. Terms, location and continued availability are not recorded here.

### Predicted protein structures aid drug design

#### Texts on the proposition

- **The Nature review "Knowledge-based prediction of protein structures and the design of novel molecules" (Blundell, Sibanda, Sternberg and Thornton, 26 March 1987) states that knowledge-based prediction techniques are of value in predicting receptor structures to aid the design of drugs.** `aib-arg-p1-blundell-1987`
  1987-03-26 · published
  _Date:_ Publication and issue date printed on the Nature article page.
  _Limits:_ The record documents what this text states, in its register; it is not evidence that the proposition is true and implies no influence on or from any other text. The method the abstract describes is knowledge-based modelling from analogies with known structures, not machine learning; the record bears on the AI period only as an earlier statement of the same expectation. Only the abstract was read. Earliest located for this proposition among the texts found by the searches in howWeSearched; not a claim that no earlier text exists.

  > “Such techniques are of value in prediction of receptor structures to aid the design of drugs, herbicides or pesticides, antigens in vaccine design, and novel molecules in protein engineering.”  
  > [Knowledge-based prediction of protein structures and the design of novel molecules](https://www.nature.com/articles/326347a0), Abstract (article page)
  > “Prediction of the tertiary structures of proteins may be carried out using a knowledge-based approach.”  
  > [Knowledge-based prediction of protein structures and the design of novel molecules](https://www.nature.com/articles/326347a0), Abstract (article page), what the techniques are

- **The Faraday Discussions paper "Modelling the structure and function of enzymes by machine learning" (Sternberg, Lewis, King and Muggleton, 1992) reports applying the machine-learning program GOLEM both to protein secondary-structure prediction and, separately, to modelling a structure-activity relationship in drug design.** `aib-arg-p1-sternberg-1992`
  1992 · published

  _Limits:_ The record documents what this text states, in its register; it is not evidence that the proposition is true and implies no influence on or from any other text. Eleven authors are employees of Insilico Medicine, which the paper names as the study sponsor; the description of the molecule as AI-generated is the authors' own. The paper's description of its trial as "first" is the authors' wording and is not adopted here.

  > “a first-in-class AI-generated small-molecule inhibitor of TNIK, a first-in-class target in idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (IPF) discovered using generative AI.”  
  > [A generative AI-discovered TNIK inhibitor for idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis: a randomized phase 2a trial](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41591-025-03743-2), Abstract
  > “are employees of Insilico Medicine. Insilico Medicine was the study sponsor.”  
  > [A generative AI-discovered TNIK inhibitor for idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis: a randomized phase 2a trial](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41591-025-03743-2), Competing interests

- **Insilico Medicine’s announcement of 8 July 2026 states that rentosertib, which it says was discovered and developed with the support of its generative AI platform, had entered a Phase III clinical trial.** `aib-arg-p3-insilico-2026`
  2026-07-08 · announced

  _Limits:_ The record documents what this text states, in its register; it is not evidence that the proposition is true and implies no influence on or from any other text. The company’s own statement; no clinical-trial registry entry was retrieved. Added by the integrator from the companies pass’s evidence (record aib-co-insilico-rentosertib-phase3-initiation-2026).
  > “Rentosertib (ISM001-055), independently discovered and developed by Insilico Medicine, has officially entered Phase III clinical trial (CTR20262475, NCT07687459)”  
  > [InSilico Medicine Cayman TopCo: Voluntary Announcement - Initiation of Insilico Medicine's Rentosertib Phase III Clinical Trial](https://www1.hkexnews.hk/listedco/listconews/sehk/2026/0708/2026070800129.pdf), first paragraph
  > “discovered and developed with the support of Insilico Medicine’s proprietary generative AI platform, Pharma. AI.”  
  > [InSilico Medicine Cayman TopCo: Voluntary Announcement - Initiation of Insilico Medicine's Rentosertib Phase III Clinical Trial](https://www1.hkexnews.hk/listedco/listconews/sehk/2026/0708/2026070800129.pdf), About Rentosertib

### AI-discovered drug candidates succeed in clinical trials more often than historical averages

#### Texts on the proposition

- **Jayatunga et al. (Drug Discovery Today, published online 30 April 2024) state that, in their analysis, AI-discovered molecules have an 80–90% success rate in phase I, substantially higher than historic industry averages, and a phase II success rate of about 40%, comparable to those averages, on a limited sample.** `aib-arg-p6-jayatunga-2024`
  2024-04-30 · published
  _Date:_ Electronic publication date as given by Europe PMC.
  _Limits:_ The record documents what this text states, in its register; it is not evidence that the proposition is true and implies no influence on or from any other text. Read in the Europe PMC record of the abstract only; the sample, the definition of "AI-discovered" and the comparison data were not read. The authors frame the result as early signs. Earliest located for this proposition among the texts found by the searches in howWeSearched; not a claim that no earlier text exists.

  > “In Phase I we find AI-discovered molecules have an 80-90% success rate, substantially higher than historic industry averages.”  
  > [How successful are AI-discovered drugs in clinical trials? A first analysis and emerging lessons](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=TITLE%3A%22How%20successful%20are%20AI-discovered%20drugs%22&format=json&resultType=core), Abstract (Europe PMC record, abstractText)
  > in phase II the authors find a success rate of about 40%, on a limited sample, which they call comparable to historic industry averages. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [How successful are AI-discovered drugs in clinical trials? A first analysis and emerging lessons](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=TITLE%3A%22How%20successful%20are%20AI-discovered%20drugs%22&format=json&resultType=core), Abstract (Europe PMC record, abstractText)

- **Xu et al. (Nature Medicine, published online 3 June 2025) state that few AI-discovered or AI-designed drugs have reached clinical trials, that AI-discovered drugs have experienced similar levels of phase 2 failure as non-AI-discovered drugs, and that none has so far progressed through phase 3.** `aib-arg-p6-xu-2025`
  2025-06-03 · published
  _Date:_ Online publication date printed on the article page.
  _Limits:_ The record documents what this text states, in its register; it is not evidence that the proposition is true and implies no influence on or from any other text. The introduction of a sponsor-authored trial report, citing other literature (its references 20 and 21, not retrieved) for these statements.
  > “AI-discovered drugs have experienced similar levels of phase 2 trial failure as non-AI-discovered drugs20,21, and none has so far progressed through phase 3 trials21.”  
  > [A generative AI-discovered TNIK inhibitor for idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis: a randomized phase 2a trial](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41591-025-03743-2), Introduction

## How we searched

**claims.**

_scope:_ Published texts on six propositions about AI and biology (P1 to P6): papers and their abstracts, perspectives, journal news articles, company announcements and press releases. Developer model papers are the subject of the `models` pass and company statements of the `companies` pass; this pass records only their stance on a proposition. Biosecurity propositions are left to the ai-bio-pack.

_routes:_ Europe PMC REST search (https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=…&format=json), lite results sorted by publication date ascending, then core records (resultType=core) for abstracts and dates; Europe PMC full-text XML (/PMC…/fullTextXML) where open; Article pages on nature.com (Nature, Nature Methods, Nature Medicine, Nature Reviews Drug Discovery, Scientific Reports), retrieved directly; arXiv abstract pages (arxiv.org/abs/…v1) and the bioRxiv details API (api.biorxiv.org/details/biorxiv/<doi>); Company sites retrieved directly: deepmind.google, isomorphiclabs.com (news listing and articles), evolutionaryscale.ai, sumitomo-pharma.com, insilico.com (news listing, to locate release addresses); Internet Archive availability API for one capture (deepmind.com, December 2020); Candidate lookup by title or DOI: several texts (Wong et al. 2022, Terwilliger et al. 2023, Lyu et al. 2024, Ma et al. 2018, Ahlmann-Eltze et al. 2025, Jayatunga et al. 2024, Xu et al. 2025, Huang et al. 2016, Anishchenko et al. 2020/2021, Watson et al. 2023, Bunne et al. 2024) were looked up as candidates by title or DOI rather than found by the term searches below; every record rests on the text then retrieved. Such lookups find what is already known and bias the earliest-located roles toward well-cited texts.

_termsAndYears:_ P1: Europe PMC: TITLE_ABS "structure prediction" AND "drug design", 1970–1995 (3 hits); ABSTRACT "structure prediction" AND drug, 1970–1994 (2 hits); TITLE "prediction of protein structures", 1970–1990 (2 hits); abstract phrases "predicted structure", "structure prediction", "predicting the structure", "tertiary structure" with "drug design" or "design of drugs", 1960–1987 (0 hits, which shows phrase matching is exact and that Blundell et al. 1987 was found only through the title search); TITLE AlphaFold AND "drug discovery", 2020–2023 (6 hits). DeepMind's 2020 account was read because the dataset already holds it. P2: Europe PMC: TITLE_ABS "virtual cell", 1960–2001 (18 hits; the earliest is a 1993 Trends in Genetics title by Casini with no abstract, not read); "virtual cell" with machine learning, deep learning, artificial intelligence or neural network, to 2021 (2 hits, neither on the criterion); "virtual cell", "whole-cell model", "model of a cell" or "simulate cell" with deep learning, neural network or artificial intelligence, MEDLINE, 1990–2023 (43 hits, none earlier than Ma et al. 2018 on the criterion); TITLE "virtual cell challenge" (3 hits). The combined search did not return Ma et al. 2018, which was looked up by its title as a candidate and then retrieved; searches for "in silico cell" and "cell simulation" with machine learning were not run, so the P2 earliest-located role is weakly bounded. P3: No bibliographic search was run for company statements: the Sumitomo Dainippon Pharma and Exscientia release of January 2020 and Insilico Medicine's statements were located from the companies' own newsrooms. Earlier company statements that a named AI-discovered candidate began a trial (for example from companies founded before 2015) were not searched; press-release archives (Business Wire, PR Newswire, GlobeNewswire) were not searched systematically, and GlobeNewswire refused the one release tried. P4: Europe PMC: TITLE_ABS "protein design" with neural network, machine learning, deep learning or artificial intelligence, to 2018 (39 hits, mostly stability prediction and engineering of natural proteins, none meeting the criterion); TITLE "protein design" with deep, neural, machine learning or generative, 2015–2020 (9 hits). The earliest located (Anishchenko et al., bioRxiv v1 of 23 July 2020) is bounded: preprints on generative models for protein design from 2019 (gcWGAN, autoregressive generative models; Europe PMC preprint records) were listed but not read, and could meet the criterion. P5: Europe PMC: abstract phrases "biology is an informational science", "biology has become an information science", "biology is an information science" (12 hits; Hood and Galas 2003 read). No search was run for "programmable biology", "digital biology" or "language of life" with machine learning, and no book or lecture literature was searched; the earliest located (Isomorphic Labs, 2021) is weakly bounded. P6: Located through the companies and trial texts above (Jayatunga et al. 2024 by title search in Europe PMC). No systematic search for analyses of AI-discovered drug success rates was run; later analyses (2025–2026) may exist.

_gaps:_ Paywalled article bodies were not read: Nature (1987, 2003, 2016), Nature Methods (2018) and the Nature and Nature Reviews Drug Discovery news articles were read in their abstracts or standfirsts only. Five texts (Sternberg 1992, Tomita 2001, Lyu 2024, Jayatunga 2024, Roohani 2025) were read only in Europe PMC records of their abstracts.; science.org was not tried (refused per the brief); the PMC page for Lyu et al. returned a browser check and the Europe PMC full text returned HTTP 500; the Elsevier page for Jayatunga et al. returned no readable text; cell.com was not tried.; Europe PMC abstract coverage before the mid-1970s is thin, so P1 and P2 searches cannot show earlier texts; no book, conference proceedings (for example ISMB, Pacific Symposium on Biocomputing) or pre-1987 review literature was searched.; No non-English text was searched; Chinese-language texts on these propositions (for example from BioMap or XtalPi) were not looked for.; Interviews, talks, op-eds and general press were not searched; DeepMind's and Isomorphic Labs' other posts were listed but only the two recorded were read.; The pages of DeepMind (2020), Isomorphic Labs (2021) and EvolutionaryScale (2024) were read as served on 2026-10-30; their wording is attested from that date. The December 2020 Internet Archive capture of DeepMind's account returned HTTP 403.

_earliestLocated:_ P1: Blundell, Sibanda, Sternberg and Thornton, Nature, 26 March 1987 (knowledge-based modelling, not machine learning). P2: Ma et al., Nature Methods, 5 March 2018. P3: Sumitomo Dainippon Pharma and Exscientia release, 30 January 2020. P4: Anishchenko et al., bioRxiv v1, 23 July 2020. P5: Isomorphic Labs, "Introducing Isomorphic Labs", 1 November 2021 (wording attested 2026-10-30). P6: Jayatunga et al., Drug Discovery Today, 30 April 2024.

_retrievalFailures:_ See `retrievalFailures`.

_note:_ "Earliest located" means the earliest text these searches found that meets the proposition's criterion and that was retrieved and checked here. It is not a claim that no earlier text exists, and it says nothing about influence between the texts. Searches ran on 2026-10-30.

## Biosecurity links

Candidates of the AI and biosecurity pack (branch `ai-bio-pack`, not admitted) that bear on the models and institutions recorded here. They are linked, not re-recorded; the sibling identifiers are pinned by commit in `sibling-ids.json`. The second column is the sibling record’s own statement or the proposition’s name.

| ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- |
| `ab-prop-design-tools-distinct`             | Biological design tools pose risks distinct from those of language models                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | The sibling pack's proposition B4: AI tools built to design or predict biological molecules or systems pose misuse risks distinct from language models. The models recorded in this pack (structure prediction, protein design, protein and genomic language models) are the kind of tool the proposition is about; this pack records no position on it. |                 |
| `ab-arg-b4-sandbrink-articulates`           | Sandbrink's preprint (arXiv v1, 24 June 2023) differentiates large language models from biological design tools, stating that language models in particular lower barriers to biological misuse while design tools expand the capabilities of sophisticated actors.                                                                                                                                                                         | A text that articulates B4 (Sandbrink, arXiv v1, 24 June 2023).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |                 |
| `ab-arg-b4-moulange-articulates`            | The preprint "Towards Responsible Governance of Biological Design Tools" (arXiv v1, 27 November 2023) states that biological design tools pose distinct regulatory challenges and that regulatory proposals tailored to large language models may be less effective for them.                                                                                                                                                               | A text that articulates B4 (preprint of 27 November 2023 on governance of biological design tools).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |                 |

## Omissions

Listed with reasons. An omission is not a judgement of importance.

- **Insilico Medicine's January 2023 release on the phase 1 results of INS018_055 (GlobeNewswire)** (claims). HTTP 403; the Nature Medicine 2025 trial report was used for Insilico instead.
- **Arnold (2023) and Mullard (2021) news bodies, and the eLife study by Karelina, Noh and Dror (2023) cited by Arnold** (claims). Paywalled or not retrieved; only standfirsts recorded.
- **Casini, "In vivo, in vitro, in silicio: towards a virtual cell" (Trends in Genetics, 1993); Schaff and Loew, "The virtual cell" (Pacific Symposium on Biocomputing, 1999)** (claims). Early uses of the name "virtual cell" for simulation, without an AI element; the 1993 item has no abstract and was not read. No earliest-located-term role was assigned, since the name's earliest use was not the object of a full search.
- **Isomorphic Labs' 2026 post on joining the Virtual Biology Initiative** (claims). Read; it concerns data for predictive AI models of biology but states no virtual-cell proposition in the readable text.
- **Statements on AI drug discovery by Recursion, insitro, Generate Biomedicines, XtalPi and BioMap** (claims). Left to the companies pass; P3 can take links to its records by id once its draft exists.
- **Third-party quotations printed on DeepMind's 2020 account (for example on accelerating drug discovery)** (claims). Relays of other speakers on a developer page; not recorded as the developer's stance.
- **BioMap founding date and the BioMap-Sanofi collaboration** (companies). BioMap's site text, as served on 2026-10-30, states no founding year; no Sanofi or BioMap press release on the collaboration could be retrieved (see retrievalFailures). Nothing is recorded from memory.
- **insitro Series A and Series B rounds; the Gilead collaboration (2019)** (companies). No insitro release for them was located on the insitro press page within the time budget; the Gilead item there links to press coverage, not an insitro release. The Series C release mentions the Series B "in mid-2020" without an amount.
- **Generate Biomedicines' private rounds (Series A-C amounts) and the Zealand Pharma and Roswell Park items** (companies). The prospectus refers to the preferred-stock series without round totals in the passages read; the recent website announcements were not retrieved. The IPO terms and two collaborations are recorded instead.
- **Recursion's later roles, the 2024 Exscientia scheme document and the Recursion 10-K founding text** (companies). The IPO prospectus already states the founding month and the CEO role; the completion is recorded from Recursion's 8-K exhibit. Additional filings would duplicate.
- **Roche Group statements on its AI groups; Genentech's 2026 "Lab in the Loop" story page** (companies). Roche's own pages were not searched within the budget. The 2026 Genentech story (published_at 2026-08-24 in its metadata) was retrieved but its body is in script data; the 2021 story and the 2023 release were preferred.
- **XtalPi's 2025-2026 HKEX updates on DoveTree and other partnerships (GPCR partner, Stanford, BASF)** (companies). Listed on HKEXnews but not read; the June 2025 term sheet and the November 2025 Ailux-Lilly collaboration are recorded as examples. The later DoveTree updates may state a definitive agreement and should be read before admission.
- **Insilico Medicine founding, leadership and earlier trial statements (Phase I and Phase IIa of rentosertib; ISM6331, ISM5411, ISM8969)** (companies). Bounded selection: the two Phase III statements and the listing are recorded. The prospectus (HKEXnews 2025-12-18) was not read.
- **Sumitomo Dainippon Pharma's own 2020 statement on DSP-1181** (companies). Not retrieved; Exscientia's 2021 prospectus statement is recorded instead and does not name DSP-1181 in the passage quoted.
- **Clinical-trial registry entries (NCT07687459, CTR20262475, SOLAIRIA trials)** (companies). Registry records are separate sources with their own dates; none was retrieved. Every trial record here is the company's own statement.
- **EvolutionaryScale founding date, founder roster and its 2024 seed round** (labs). No primary statement was retrieved: the company site now serves only the ESM3 post, a model post and a home page (no news, about or team pages: HTTP 404), and the press release reported elsewhere could not be located by direct retrieval. Secondary aggregator pages found during discovery were not used as evidence. Recorded only what the company and Biohub state.
- **The 2021 Isomorphic Labs launch article's statement that its author would serve as CEO while remaining DeepMind CEO** (labs). The page as served names no author; the role is recorded instead from the January 2024 release that names Hassabis as CEO and founder. A contemporaneous 2021 copy could not be opened (Internet Archive capture blocked).
- **Pushmeet Kohli's Google DeepMind science role** (labs). No Google DeepMind page retrieved in this pass states it; left to the people pass.
- **Google DeepMind's AlphaProteo announcement, AlphaFold 3 code and weights release, and AlphaFold DB coverage figures** (labs). Assigned to the models pass by the brief. DeepMind's 2022 statement of the AlphaFold DB expansion and its 2021 initial count were drafted and then cut to avoid overlap with database records.
- **Other Isomorphic Labs leaders and its Scientific Advisory Board (team page, 2026-10-30)** (labs). Bounded selection: CEO and President only, plus the announced change of President.
- **Arc Institute founding donors, Virtual Cell Challenge 2026 data-generation account and Evo pages** (labs). Donor names are listed without amounts and add no stated funding; the 2026 data post is laboratory-method material outside the hazard-safe register; Evo belongs to the models pass.
- **IPD spinout figures, the Amgen-IPD 2019 partnership and other IPD collaborations** (labs). Bounded selection; the spinout figures were drafted and cut as company-level figures not verified here; the Amgen release was found in discovery but not retrieved.
- **NVIDIA BioNeMo 2023 expansion (Amgen and others named as users), the 2024 and 2026 platform descriptions and the Thermo Fisher collaboration** (labs). Bounded selection; the 2023 release was retrieved and its records cut for length. The 2026 Lilly co-innovation lab is kept as the one stated partnership.
- **Microsoft Research's Novartis Generative Chemistry collaboration (stated in the 2022 post)** (labs). Drafted and cut: stated in passing by one party, not an announcement of the collaboration.
- **Isomorphic Labs' stated headcount (over 300 employees, November 2025) and the aggregate "nearly $3 billion" potential value of the 2024 collaborations** (labs). Cut for length; the aggregate is the company's own potential figure and the individual deal terms are recorded instead.
- **AlphaFold 2 statements already recorded in the dataset (that the paper addresses sequence-to-structure prediction, Jumper's authorship and affiliation, TPU v3 training) and in the technical-history candidate th-tl-alphafold-2021 (validation in CASP14)** (models). Already recorded; this pass adds only the CASP14 accuracy figures, two stated limitations and the code-availability statement, and names the existing records in extends and siblingLinks.
- **The CASP13 and CASP14 category assessment papers in Proteins (template-based modelling, free modelling, assembly, refinement, contact prediction) and the per-target result tables at predictioncenter.org** (models). Out of the bounded selection: the pass records the organisers' summary assessments, not per-category or per-target scores. A later increment could add one assessor paper per category.
- **CASP15, CASP16 and CASP17 results** (models). The Prediction Center home page states that CASP17 completed in August 2026, but the assessment papers for CASP15 to CASP17 were not retrieved, and the home page's narrative panels mix rounds; recording them needs the organisers' overview papers and the assessors' own papers.
- **The AlphaFold 2 preprint and conference expressions, the AlphaFold-Multimer paper, AlphaFold Server, AlphaGenome and AlphaProteo's technical report** (models). Not retrieved in this pass. The AlphaProteo technical report in particular would need a hazard review before any statement beyond the announcement's policy-level passages.
- **ESM-2 and AlphaMissense full texts, and the ESM3 and Evo 2 preprint full texts** (models). science.org refuses direct retrieval and neither Science article is in PubMed Central; the PubMed records were used for the abstract, author list and dates, and the preprints through their repository metadata. Their stated limitations therefore are not recorded.
- **ESM Metagenomic Atlas pages, the ESM GitHub releases, the Evo and Evo 2 repositories and model cards** (models). Not retrieved; the pass records the papers' and announcements' own statements about releases rather than the artefacts.
- **Design examples, generated proteins, benchmark tables, prompts and all method detail in the ESM3, Evo, Evo 2, RFdiffusion and AlphaProteo texts** (models). Hazard-safe scope: only policy-level, dataset-level and publication-level statements are recorded, and each affected record carries a hazardNote.
- **Author email addresses printed in the ESM-1b, Evo, RoseTTAFold and CASP papers, and the corresponding-author addresses on the AlphaFold database papers** (models). Privacy: addresses were visible in the retrieved texts and were deliberately not copied into any field.
- **Rosetta's own founding documents and the Rosetta software licence terms** (models). Only the Rosetta Commons history page was retrieved; the page is undated and its "around 2003-2005" is approximate. A licensing record would need the licence documents themselves.
- **Demis Hassabis as 'CEO of Google DeepMind' in the Nobel press release (9 October 2024) and as 'Co-founder and CEO' on Google DeepMind's About page (served 2026-10-30)** (people). The dataset already records his Google DeepMind CEO role (2023) and the August 2026 Chair announcement, whose qualification already notes the About page caption; the brief asks for new roles only.
- **Hassabis as CEO of Isomorphic Labs (Google DeepMind's Nobel post of 9 October 2024; AlphaFold 3 affiliation list)** (people). Isomorphic Labs leadership belongs to the labs pass.
- **David Baker as Director of the Institute for Protein Design (IPD page, retrieved as people-ipd-baker, sha256 fbb09c51…cba1)** (people). IPD's director is in the labs pass's brief (institution leaders). The page is cached for the labs pass to use; it prints email addresses, which must not be copied.
- **Rives's EvolutionaryScale role, Hsu's Arc Institute role, Koller's insitro role, Gibson's Recursion role** (people). Company and institute leadership roles belong to the labs and companies passes (brief).
- **Pushmeet Kohli's Google DeepMind title** (people). No Google DeepMind page retrieved states it (About, Science, AlphaProteo post, Nobel post and the November 2024 essay 'A new golden age of discovery' were searched for 'Kohli'; the essay lists him as an author without a title). Left for the labs pass or a later search.
- **Kohli's authorship of 'A new golden age of discovery' (Google DeepMind, November 2024)** (people). Not one of the pack's documents; the claims pass may use the essay. Cached as people-gdm-ai-for-science.
- **Laureates' birth years and places, PhD details and the prize amount line in the Nobel release** (people). Privacy rule (no personal details beyond a role record); the amount is not a role or award proposition the pack needs.
- **The Nobel release's characterisation of the work ('solve a 50-year-old problem', 'stunning breakthrough', usage figures)** (people). Claims about the work, not about people; left to the claims pass, which must quote and attribute those words.
- **The Nobel scientific background document** (people). Not needed for the award records; the press release is the awarding body's statement of the decision and citations.
- **Authorship of John Moult on CASP assessment papers, and other co-authors in the registry on papers not listed here (e.g. Jumper on AlphaFold DB papers)** (people). The models pass had not defined those documents when this draft was written; bounded selection.
- **Paper affiliations as held_role snapshots (e.g. Baek at Seoul National University, Rives at FAIR/NYU, Hayes at EvolutionaryScale)** (people). Printed affiliation is not employment; the brief records them only where needed. Mentioned in qualifications only.
- **Krzysztof Fidelis as a separate co_founded record** (people). Not in the shared registry; named in the Moult record's statement and evidence. Can be added with a new entity if wanted.

- https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC11253030/fullTextXML — HTTP 500. Fallback: Europe PMC core record (abstract). (claims)
- https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11253030/ — HTTP 200 with a browser-check page (23 words). Fallback: Europe PMC core record (abstract). (claims)

## Open questions for the maintainer

- P1's criterion is not limited to AI, so its earliest located text (Blundell et al. 1987) is knowledge-based modelling. Keep the criterion broad, or narrow it to machine-learning predictions? _Recommended:_ Keep it broad: the expectation that predicted structures aid drug design predates machine-learning structure prediction, which is what the record shows; the qualification says the 1987 method is not machine learning. (claims)
- Should records resting only on a Europe PMC abstract record (five texts) be admitted, or held until the article itself is read? _Recommended:_ Admit as abstract-level records with the source labelled as the Europe PMC record, and reread at the publisher where access allows; the earliest-located role for P6 (Jayatunga et al.) should be rechecked against the article before admission. (claims)
- Ahlmann-Eltze et al. (2025) are recorded as contesting P2 (present-tense half only). Is "contests" right, or should it be context? _Recommended:_ Contests, with the qualifier as written: the paper disputes, for the tested models, the capability the criterion states in its present tense; the cross-family check should look at this record. (claims)
- The pages of Isomorphic Labs (2021) and EvolutionaryScale (2024) name or omit authors differently from the labs pass's possible records of the same pages. Who owns these documents? _Recommended:_ The labs pass, if it defines them; the integrator should merge the document ids (aib-doc-isomorphic-intro-2021, aib-doc-evolutionaryscale-esm3-post-2024) into its ids. (claims)
- NVIDIA's $50 million PIPE in Recursion is one investor's stated amount, not a round total. The draft used scope "investor_amount", which is not a schema value (the schema allows transaction, round_total and cumulative). Which scope should an admitted record use? _Recommended:_ Resolved on 2026-10-30 by the audit dispositions (family decision b): scope "transaction", the schema's value for a single transaction, as in the dataset's comparable records. (companies)
- participated_in_round for CPP Investments carries attributes.participation: "lead", which the schema may not have. _Recommended:_ Keep "led" in the statement and drop the attribute if the schema has no participation role. (companies)
- Collaborations stated in offering documents (Generate's Amgen and Novartis agreements, XtalPi's Pfizer account) are recorded as states_provision on the prospectus, not as partnership_announced, because the prospectus states agreements entered and payments received rather than announcing them. Is that the right split? _Recommended:_ Yes: keep partnership_announced for announcements and states_provision for an issuer's later account, so announced and realised terms stay distinct. (companies)
- The Genentech 2021 story on Prescient Design is cited by paraphrase because its body is in script data. Should the pack accept a paraphrase-only acquisition record? _Recommended:_ Accept it as a candidate, flagged for the audit; before admission, prefer a Roche or Genentech document whose text layer can be quote-checked (for example Roche's 2021 annual report). (companies)
- BioMap's text is taken from the site's JavaScript bundle, the only form the site serves. Is a bundle an acceptable expression of a web page? _Recommended:_ Yes, recorded as the page text as served on the retrieval date, with the bundle file name as the version; re-check against a rendered capture before admission. (companies)
- XtalPi's listed issuer was QuantumPharm Inc. until 8 November 2024. Should a separate entity be created for the earlier name, with name_transition_announced? _Recommended:_ No: keep one entity (aib-xtalpi) and the states_provision record of the name change; the dataset uses name_transition_announced for distinct institutional referents, and this is a registered-name change of the same issuer. (companies)
- Google DeepMind's blog pages for the 2018 AlphaFold post and the 2021 AlphaFold DB post now show dates of 15 January 2022 and 22 July 2022, which conflict with their texts. Should the pack date them by inference (2018; July 2021) as drafted, or wait for an opened archive capture? _Recommended:_ Keep the inferred dates with their rationale and mark the records for the cross-family check; replace them with the capture dates (2018-12-03 and 2021-07-24 per the availability API) only after a capture is opened and its text matches. (labs)
- partnership_announced is a proposed predicate; the Lilly and Novartis records carry an extra attributes.milestones object (up_to) beside attributes.amount (upfront). Is that shape acceptable, or should milestones stay in the statement only? _Recommended:_ Resolved on 2026-10-30 by the audit dispositions (family decision b): attributes.milestones removed from both records; the milestone eligibility stays in the statement and qualification, and the upfront amount carries the schema scope "transaction". (labs)
- How should "EvolutionaryScale team joins Biohub" (November 2025) be modelled: as a statement only (as drafted), or with combined_into or acquired? _Recommended:_ Statement only. Neither party's text calls it an acquisition or a combination of entities; it is a team joining under a "strategic transaction with the Chan Zuckerberg Initiative". (labs)
- Is the Microsoft Research AI for Science lab (2026 page) the same unit as the AI4Science team announced in 2022? _Recommended:_ Treat as one candidate entity (aib-msr-ai-for-science) on the shared name and the 2022 post's link to the lab; note the name change and the different stated locations (2022: UK, China, Netherlands; 2026: UK, Netherlands, Germany) and revisit if Microsoft states a reorganisation. (labs)
- Three records rest on PubMed records rather than the journal expression (AlphaMissense, ESM-2, ESM3; also the 1997 Rosetta paper). Is a PubMed record an acceptable expression for a paper's stated contribution, given that the abstract text is the authors' own as reproduced by the National Library of Medicine? _Recommended:_ Accept for the abstract-level statements and dates recorded here, with the source entry naming the expression and the qualification saying the full text was not read; replace with the journal expression if access is ever available. Do not add further records from these papers without the full text. (models)
- Both AlphaFold database papers state different figures for the 2021 release (over 360,000 in the 2022 paper, "the initial 300k structures" in the 2024 paper), and the About page states over 200 million where the 2024 paper states over 214 million. _Recommended:_ Keep all four as separate dated statements, as drafted, and let the discrepancy stand on the record; do not reconcile them into one number. (models)
- The abstract of the CASP organisers' CASP14 overview paper characterises the results as "a solution to the classical protein folding problem, at least for single proteins". The pack records this as their evaluative wording. _Recommended:_ Keep it as a quoted and attributed characterisation in Models and resources, and let the claims pass build any proposition on it rather than restating it elsewhere. (models)
- One negative record (aib-mod-rfdiffusion-no-biosecurity-section) states that no biosecurity or dual-use passage was found in the RFdiffusion article page, bounded by the searched terms and excluding the Supplementary Information. _Recommended:_ Keep, since the brief asks for hazardNote treatment of papers with biosecurity sections and the absence is itself documentary; but have the audit re-run the same search terms, and note that the Supplementary Information was not read. (models)
- Document ids for papers the people pass must reference were fixed from the brief's list. Two further ids were needed: aib-doc-esm3-preprint-2024 and aib-doc-evo2-preprint-2025 for the preprint expressions, plus aib-doc-afdb-* and aib-doc-rosetta-*. _Recommended:_ Adopt them as drafted; the people pass should record authorship on the journal expressions (aib-doc-esm3-2025, aib-doc-evo-2024) and not on the preprint metadata records, whose author lists come from a repository API and are truncated for Evo 2. (models)
- award_announced is a proposed predicate (subject the Academy, object a person). Is it acceptable, or should the award be modelled differently (e.g. a states_provision on the press release)? _Recommended:_ Accept award_announced as proposed; it keeps the awarding body as the stating party and the citation in attributes. (people)
- The Nobel release calls Jumper 'Senior Research Scientist at Google DeepMind'; Google DeepMind's post of the same day (dataset atlas-jumper-gdm) calls him 'Director'. Keep both? _Recommended:_ Keep both as separately sourced snapshots; do not reconcile them into one title. (people)
- Authorship records for ESM-2, ESM3, Evo and AlphaMissense rest on PubMed records (secondary bibliographic metadata) because the Science articles could not be retrieved. Acceptable for admission? _Recommended:_ Acceptable as interim evidence marked secondary; replace with the article's own author list if the models pass retrieves an expression of the article (e.g. Europe PMC or a publisher-hosted PDF). (people)
- Moult's 'CASP chair and founder' and Baker's advisory-board role come from the undated CASP14 page; dated here at year precision (2020) because the page describes CASP14. Acceptable, or should they be snapshots at the retrieval date? _Recommended:_ Keep 2020 at year precision with the served date in the source; the page is CASP14's own and its committee belongs to that round. (people)
- co_founded points at aib-casp (the Protein Structure Prediction Center), while the page's 'founder' refers to CASP the experiment series. Is a separate entity for the CASP experiments wanted? _Recommended:_ No separate entity for now; the qualification states the distinction. (people)
- 44 records exceeds the brief's aim of about 30, mainly through authorship records. Trim? _Recommended:_ Keep; each authorship record is a single credited name on one paper. If trimming is wanted, drop the AlphaMissense set (secondary evidence) first. (people)
